### Q35: What is true of cystic hygroma? It arises from sweat glands in the neck. It is usually an anterior midline structure. It may occur inthe posterior triangle of the neck. Its lesions are usually easy to enucleate. It is premalignant

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 6
**EXP:** Cystic hygroma is a congenital macrocystic lymphatic malformation that arises from sequestered lymphatic sacs. It is characteristically soft, compressible, painless, and displays brilliant translucence on transillumination.

### Q252: The surface area of the peritoneal membrane is nearly equal to that of the skin

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. The surface area of the peritoneal membrane in an adult is approximately 1.7 to 2.0 square meters, which is roughly equivalent to the total cutaneous surface area.

---

### Q253: The parietal peritoneum is poorly innervated

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** False. The parietal peritoneum is richly innervated by somatic afferent nerves (intercostal and subcostal nerves, phrenic nerve) and is exquisitely sensitive to sharp, localized pain, pressure, and temperature.

---

### Q254: The peritoneum has the capacity to absorb large volumes of fluid

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. The peritoneum has a remarkable absorptive capacity (absorbing up to several liters of fluid per day), primarily via lymphatic stomata located on the peritoneal surface of the diaphragm.

---

### Q255: The peritoneum has the ability to produce fibrinolytic activity

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. Normal peritoneal mesothelial cells produce tissue plasminogen activator (tPA), providing intrinsic fibrinolytic activity to prevent permanent fibrous adhesion formation; this activity decreases during inflammation and ischemia.

---

### Q256: The pain never radiates to the back and this differentiates this from biliary colic F

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** False. In acute pancreatitis or peptic ulcer perforation, pain frequently radiates straight through to the back (at T10-L2 levels), whereas biliary colic pain typically radiates to the right infrascapular area.

---

### Q257: Vomiting is a notable feature

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. Vomiting is a prominent early clinical sign in high small bowel mechanical obstruction and acute peritonitis, often following the onset of severe abdominal pain.

---

### Q258: Bleeding is rare

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. In simple uncomplicated mechanical bowel obstruction, intraluminal bleeding is rare unless vascular strangulation, mucosal infarction, or an underlying ulcerated malignancy is present.

---

### Q259: They may cause gastric outlet obstruction T

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. Chronic peptic ulcer disease in the pyloric channel or duodenal bulb, as well as prepyloric gastric adenocarcinoma, are major causes of gastric outlet obstruction due to cicatricial stenosis or mechanical blockage.

---

### Q260: Weightloss is a typical symptom

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. Significant unintentional weight loss is a hallmark symptom of malignant gastrointestinal tumors, chronic mesenteric ischemia, and advanced gastric outlet obstruction.

---

### Q261: Pancreatic injury is common following blunt ab dominal trauma F

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** False. The retroperitoneal location of the pancreas protects it, making traumatic injury relatively uncommon (occurring in fewer than 5-10% of blunt abdominal trauma cases), typically resulting from severe compressive force against the vertebral column.

---

### Q262: Pancreatic injury is often accompanied by damage to the liver, spleen and duodenum T

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. Because high force is required to injure the retroperitoneal pancreas, associated injuries to contiguous organs (liver, spleen, duodenum, and major abdominal vessels) occur in over 70% of cases.

---

### Q263: the serum amylase is raised in most cases of pancreatic injury T

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. Serum amylase and lipase levels are elevated in approximately 70-80% of patients with significant pancreatic parenchyma or ductal trauma, although initial normal levels do not rule out injury.

---

### Q264: It is most common in children with a peak incidence between 5 and 10 months of age

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. Idiopathic ileocolic intussusception has its peak clinical incidence between 5 and 10 months of age, coinciding with the introduction of solid foods and adenovirus-associated lymphoid hyperplasia.

---

### Q265: About 10 per cent of infantile intussusceptions are idiopathic

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** False. Over 90% of intussusceptions occurring in infants and young children under 2 years of age are idiopathic (due to hypertrophied Peyer patches), with identifiable anatomical lead points found in only about 5-10%.

---

### Q266: It causes obstruction but not strangulation

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** False. Intussusception causes progressive mechanical obstruction and, if untreated, leads to venous congestion, ischemia, bowel wall necrosis, strangulation, perforation, and peritonitis.

---

### Q267: Meckel's diverticulum can be a cause in older children.

**Correct Answer:** -
**Answer Source:** derived
**Source Pages:** 31
**EXP:** True. In children older than 2 years and adults, intussusception is most commonly caused by an identifiable pathological lead point, of which Meckel's diverticulum and polyps are the most frequent.

---

- **D)** Intracardiac adrenaline.
